    62	  }
    63	  const f = FLOORS[platform];
    64	  if (f.floor && cmpVersions(v, f.floor) < 0) {
    65	    return { id, ok: false, detail: `${platform} ${v} is below the supported floor ${f.label} — lifecycle hooks unavailable; the adapter degrades to protocol-only (Tier C)` };
    66	  }
    67	  if (cmpVersions(v, TESTED[platform]) > 0) {
    68	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} is newer than tested (${TESTED[platform]}) — untested; signature drift is absorbed by the overlay table` };
    69	  }
    70	  return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} (tested ${TESTED[platform]})` };
    71	}
    72	
    73	/**
    74	 * Codex/Cursor hook surface: installed / trusted / observed-executing. Trust
    75	 * is not verifiable from outside the harness, so the fidelity claim is gated
    76	 * on the CANARY — journal evidence that a hook of this platform actually ran.
    77	 * @param {any} io @param {string} root @param {any} p bundlePaths
    78	 * @param {string} platform @param {string} relPath @param {string} trustNote
    79	 */
    80	function hookSurfaceCheck(io, root, p, platform, relPath, trustNote) {
    81	  const id = `${platform}-hooks`;
    82	  let text = null;
    83	  try {
    84	    text = io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/${relPath}`, 'utf8');
    85	  } catch {
    86	    text = null;
    87	  }
    88	  if (text === null || !text.includes('baton')) {
    89	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `not installed — run 'baton init --${platform}' to write ${relPath}` };
    90	  }
    91	  let observed = false;
    92	  try {
    93	    for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
    94	      if (e?.source === platform || String(e?.writerId ?? '').startsWith(`${platform}-`)) {
    95	        observed = true;
    96	        break;
    97	      }
    98	    }
    99	  } catch {
   100	    observed = false;
   101	  }
   102	  return observed
   103	    ? { id, ok: true, detail: 'installed; execution observed (canary) — the ≤1-turn mechanical-staleness claim holds' }
   104	    : { id, ok: true, detail: `installed; execution not yet observed — ${trustNote}; the fidelity claim is gated on this canary` };
   105	}
   106	
   107	// Noninteractive status probes per platform (docs/design/platform-notes.md CLIs).
   108	// capOnSuccess caps what a clean exit PROVES (gate-2 iter-2 M3): `claude
   109	// --version` proves the binary is INSTALLED, not that the server was reached or
   110	// that auth is valid, so its capability is capped at "installed". The codex/
   111	// cursor probes are auth-checking status commands, so a clean exit legitimately
   112	// proves "reachable".
   113	const PROBES = [
   114	  { platform: 'claude-code', bin: 'claude', args: ['--version'], capOnSuccess: 'installed' },
   115	  { platform: 'codex', bin: 'codex', args: ['login', 'status'], capOnSuccess: 'reachable' },
   116	  { platform: 'cursor', bin: 'cursor-agent', args: ['status'], capOnSuccess: 'reachable' },
   117	];
   118	
   119	// Word-boundary-safe AI-attribution trailer patterns (plan §Attribution guard).
   120	const TRAILER_PATTERNS = [
   121	  /co-authored-by:[^\n]*\b(claude|gpt|copilot|codex)\b/i,
   122	  /generated (with|by)[^\n]*\bclaude\b/i,
   123	  /\u{1F916}/u,
   124	];
   125	
   126	/** @param {any} io @param {string} path @returns {string | null} */
   127	function readOrNull(io, path) {
   128	  try {
   129	    return io.fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
   130	  } catch {
   131	    return null;
   132	  }
   133	}
   134	
   135	/**
   136	 * Classify one probe invocation per the two-dimension record: capability is
   137	 * the ordered ladder verified as far as the evidence goes; outcome is what the
   138	 * probe itself did. A server-issued limit response proves authentication.
   139	 * @param {{platform: string, bin: string, args: string[], capOnSuccess?: string}} probe @param {any} io @param {any} table
   140	 */
   141	async function runProbe(probe, io, table) {
   142	  try {
   143	    await io.execFile(probe.bin, probe.args, { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
   144	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: probe.capOnSuccess ?? 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' };
   145	  } catch (err) {
   146	    const e = /** @type {any} */ (err);
   147	    if (e?.code === 'ENOENT') return { platform: probe.platform, capability: null, outcome: 'error' };
   148	    if (e?.killed === true) return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'timeout' };
   149	    const text = `${e?.stdout ?? ''}\n${e?.stderr ?? ''}`;
   150	    if (table && classify({ text, exitCode: 1, platform: probe.platform, table, structured: null }).class === 'usage-limit') {
   151	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'rate-limited' };
   152	    }
   153	    if (/authenticated as|logged in as/i.test(text) && /unreachable|ECONN|offline|network/i.test(text)) {
   154	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'error' };
   155	    }
   156	    if (/not logged in|log ?in first|unauthorized|authenticat/i.test(text)) {
   157	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   158	    }
   159	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   160	  }
   161	}
   162	
   163	/** @param {any} io @param {string} root @returns {Promise<{id: string, ok: boolean, detail?: string}>} */
   164	async function gitGuardCheck(io, root) {
   165	  let log;
   166	  try {
   167	    log = await io.execFile('git', ['log', '-n', String(GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND), '--format=%h %s%n%n%b'], { cwd: root, timeout: 5000 });
   168	  } catch {
   169	    return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: 'git unavailable — commit scan skipped' };
   170	  }
   171	  // Chunks start at a line beginning with an abbreviated sha + space.
   172	  const chunks = log.stdout.split(/^(?=[0-9a-f]{7,40} )/m).filter((/** @type {string} */ c) => c.trim() !== '');
   173	  for (const chunk of chunks) {
   174	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(chunk))) {
   175	      const sha = chunk.slice(0, chunk.indexOf(' '));
   176	      return {
   177	        id: 'git-guard',
   178	        ok: false,
   179	        detail: `commit ${sha} carries an AI attribution trailer — rewrite it before publishing (git rebase -i / git commit --amend)`,
   180	      };
   181	    }
   182	  }
   183	  return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: `no AI trailers in the last ${GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND} commits` };
   184	}
   185	
   186	/**
   187	 * `baton doctor` — machine health report: attribution settings, git-guard
   188	 * trailer scan, journal size, filesystem boundary, and per-platform probe
   189	 * records ({capability, outcome}, 3 s bound, 15-min cache). Report-only by
   190	 * default; `--strict` turns any failing check into exit 1.
   191	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
   192	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   193	 */
   194	export async function cmdDoctor(args, io) {
   195	  const { flags } = parseFlags(args);
   196	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
   197	  const p = bundlePaths(root);
   198	  /** @type {{id: string, ok: boolean, detail?: string}[]} */
   199	  const checks = [];
   200	
   201	  // Attribution settings: repo-level first, then the user's global file.
   202	  const settingsText = readOrNull(io, `${root}/.claude/settings.json`) ?? readOrNull(io, `${io.env?.HOME ?? ''}/.claude/settings.json`);
   203	  let attrOk = false;
   204	  try {
   205	    const s = settingsText === null ? null : JSON.parse(settingsText);
   206	    attrOk = s?.attribution?.commit === '' && s?.attribution?.pr === '';
   207	  } catch {
   208	    attrOk = false;
   209	  }
   210	  checks.push({
   211	    id: 'attribution-settings',
   212	    ok: attrOk,
   213	    detail: attrOk ? 'attribution.commit and attribution.pr are ""' : 'set {"attribution":{"commit":"","pr":""}} in .claude/settings.json (baton init does this)',
   214	  });
   215	
   216	  checks.push(await gitGuardCheck(io, root));
   217	
   218	  const journalText = readOrNull(io, p.journal);
   219	  const journalBytes = journalText === null ? 0 : Buffer.byteLength(journalText);
   220	  checks.push({
   221	    id: 'journal-size',
   222	    ok: journalBytes <= JOURNAL_WARN_BYTES,
   223	    detail: journalBytes > JOURNAL_WARN_BYTES ? `journal is ${(journalBytes / 1048576).toFixed(1)} MB (> 5 MB) — finalize to rotate it` : `${journalBytes} bytes`,
   224	  });
   225	
   226	
   227	  // Platform probes + version probes + mount detection: every non-git child
   228	  // invocation rides the same 15-min cache in the state dir.
   229	  const cachePath = `${p.logDir}/probe-cache.json`;
   230	  const cached = safeReadJson(io.fs, cachePath);
   231	  /** @type {any[]} */
   232	  let platforms;
   233	  /** @type {Record<string, string | null>} */
   234	  let versions;
   235	  /** @type {string | null} */
   236	  let mountSource;
   237	  if (cached.ok && typeof cached.value?.at === 'string' && Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(cached.value.at) < PROBE_CACHE_MS && Array.isArray(cached.value.records)) {
   238	    platforms = cached.value.records;
   239	    versions = cached.value.versions ?? {};
   240	    mountSource = cached.value.mount ?? null;
   241	  } else {
   242	    /** @type {any} */
   243	    let table = null;
   244	    try {
   245	      // Packaged data, not repo state: read through the real fs so probes can
   246	      // classify limit responses regardless of the injected io.fs contents.
   247	      table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, { fs: realFs });
   248	    } catch {
   249	      table = null;
   250	    }
   251	    platforms = [];
   252	    for (const probe of PROBES) platforms.push(await runProbe(probe, io, table));
   253	    versions = {};
   254	    for (const platform of Object.keys(VERSION_BINS)) versions[platform] = await versionOf(platform, io);
   255	    mountSource = null;
   256	    try {
   257	      const df = await io.execFile('df', ['-P', root], { timeout: 2000 });
   258	      mountSource = ((df.stdout ?? '').split('\n')[1] ?? '').trim().split(/\s+/)[0] || null;
   259	    } catch {
   260	      mountSource = null;
   261	    }
   262	    ensureDir(io.fs, p.logDir);
   263	    atomicWriteJson(io.fs, cachePath, { at: io.now(), records: platforms, versions, mount: mountSource });
   264	  }
   265	
   266	  // Network-filesystem detection (gate-2 major 13): a mount source that looks
   267	  // like SMB/NFS fails the check; unverifiable degrades to the static boundary.
   268	  if (mountSource === null) {
   269	    checks.push({
   270	      id: 'filesystem',
   271	      ok: true,
   272	      detail: 'mount type unverifiable — v1 supports local filesystems only; lock/rename atomicity is not guaranteed on network or cloud-sync mounts',
   273	    });
   274	  } else if (/^\/\/|@|^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+:\//.test(mountSource)) {
   275	    checks.push({
   276	      id: 'filesystem',
   277	      ok: false,
   278	      detail: `${mountSource} looks like a network mount — lock/rename atomicity is not guaranteed there; v1 supports local filesystems only`,
   279	    });
   280	  } else {
   281	    checks.push({ id: 'filesystem', ok: true, detail: `local mount (${mountSource}) — note: v1 supports local filesystems only` });
   282	  }
   283	
   284	  // Version floors + tested cap, and the hook trust/canary surfaces.
   285	  for (const platform of Object.keys(VERSION_BINS)) {
   286	    checks.push(versionCheck(platform, versions[platform] ?? null));
   287	  }
   288	  checks.push(hookSurfaceCheck(io, root, p, 'codex', '.codex/hooks.json', 'trust review may be pending (run /hooks inside Codex to trust the definitions)'));
   289	  checks.push(hookSurfaceCheck(io, root, p, 'cursor', '.cursor/hooks.json', 'project hooks require a trusted workspace in Cursor'));
   290	
   291	  const failing = checks.filter((c) => !c.ok);
   292	  if (flags.json) {
   293	    emitEnvelope(io, { ok: failing.length === 0, data: { checks, platforms }, warnings: failing.map((c) => `${c.id}: ${c.detail ?? 'failed'}`) });
   294	  } else {
   295	    for (const c of checks) io.stdout.write(`${c.ok ? 'ok  ' : 'FAIL'} ${c.id}${c.detail ? ` — ${c.detail}` : ''}\n`);
   296	    for (const r of platforms) io.stdout.write(`probe ${r.platform}: capability ${r.capability ?? 'not-installed'}, outcome ${r.outcome}\n`);
   297	  }
   298	  return flags.strict === true && failing.length > 0 ? 1 : 0;
   299	}
     1	/**
     2	 * Pure, explainable role resolution over the committed matrix. Every decision
     3	 * is auditable: chosen entries carry their chainIndex; passed-over entries land
     4	 * in skipped[] with a why. avoid[] population is the caller's job.
     5	 * @param {{config: any, to: string, avoid?: string[], nativeOnly?: boolean, probes?: Record<string, {capability: string, outcome: string}> | null}} input
     6	 * @returns {{assignments: Record<string, any>, notes: string[]}}
     7	 */
     8	export function resolveRoles({ config, to, avoid = [], nativeOnly = false, probes = null }) {
     9	  /** @type {string[]} */
    10	  const notes = [];
    11	
    12	  /**
    13	   * The eligibility verdict for a platform, or null when eligible.
    14	   * @param {string} platform @returns {string | null}
    15	   */
    16	  function skipReason(platform) {
    17	    if (avoid.includes(platform)) return 'avoided';
    18	    if (!(platform in (config.platforms ?? {}))) return 'unknown-platform';
    19	    if (config.platforms[platform]?.enabled === false) return 'disabled';
    20	    if (probes && probes[platform]) {
    21	      if (probes[platform].outcome === 'rate-limited') return 'rate-limited';
    22	      if (probes[platform].capability === 'installed') return 'unauthenticated';
    23	      if (probes[platform].capability === 'not-installed') return 'not-installed';
    24	    }
    25	    return null;
    26	  }
    27	
    28	  // A selection is degraded when its availability is UNVERIFIED (gate-2 iter-2
    29	  // M3): with no probe cache at all, resolution is offline and EVERY selection
    30	  // is degraded (plan: "Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged degraded");
    31	  // with a partial cache, only the platforms the cache is missing are.
    32	  /** @param {string} platform */
    33	  const probeUnverified = (platform) => probes == null || !probes[platform];
    34	
    35	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
    36	  const assignments = {};
    37	  for (const [role, chain] of Object.entries(config.roles)) {
    38	    /** @type {any[]} */
    39	    const skipped = [];
    40	    /** @type {any} */
    41	    let assignment = null;
    42	
    43	    for (let i = 0; i < chain.length; i += 1) {
    44	      const entry = chain[i];
    45	      if (nativeOnly && entry.platform !== to) {
    46	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why: 'non-native' });
    47	        continue;
    48	      }
    49	      const why = skipReason(entry.platform);
    50	      if (why) {
    51	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why });
    52	        continue;
    53	      }
    54	      assignment = {
    55	        platform: entry.platform,
    56	        model: entry.model,
    57	        effort: entry.effort ?? null,
    58	        mode: entry.platform === to ? 'native' : 'delegated',
    59	        chainIndex: i,
    60	        skipped,
    61	      };
    62	      if (probeUnverified(entry.platform)) assignment.degraded = true;
    63	      break;
    64	    }
    65	
    66	    if (!assignment && nativeOnly) {
    67	      // Forced-default is only valid when the destination itself is eligible.
    68	      const whyTo = skipReason(to);
    69	      const model = config.defaults?.[to];
    70	      if (!whyTo && typeof model === 'string') {
    71	        assignment = { platform: to, model, effort: null, mode: 'forced-default', chainIndex: null, skipped };
    72	        if (probeUnverified(to)) assignment.degraded = true;
    73	      }
    74	    }
    75	
    76	    if (!assignment) {
    77	      assignment = { platform: null, model: null, effort: null, mode: 'unavailable', chainIndex: null, skipped };
    78	    }
    79	    assignments[role] = assignment;
    80	  }
    81	
    82	  return { assignments, notes };
    83	}
     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
     4	import { cmdDoctor } from '../../core/src/commands/doctor.mjs';
     5	import { bundlePaths } from '../../core/src/bundle/store.mjs';
     6	
     7	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     8	// Command-level contract for core/src/commands/doctor.mjs. Direct import over a
     9	// fakeio whose execFile is REPLACED with a responder (doctor probes platforms
    10	// and scans git via the async injected execFile, so cmdDoctor is async).
    11	//
    12	// TARGET MODULE: core/src/commands/doctor.mjs (sole target — its absence is the
    13	// only reason this file is RED). doctor internally consumes git/guard.mjs,
    14	// git/snapshot.mjs and the probe layer; this file imports ONLY doctor and drives
    15	// everything through it (git/guard.mjs is NOT a Wave-D target, so it is exercised
    16	// through doctor, never imported here).
    17	//
    18	// Source of truth: plan §Role matrix "Availability probing" (doctor probes each
    19	// platform noninteractively, HARD 3s bound per probe, results cached 15 min;
    20	// probe record = two SEPARATE dimensions: capability {installed→authenticated→
    21	// reachable} recorded as far as verifiable, and outcome {ok|rate-limited|error|
    22	// timeout}); §Attribution guard layer 2 (doctor scans the LAST 50 COMMITS for AI
    23	// co-author/generated-with trailers, WORD-BOUNDARY-safe, and flags with
    24	// remediation); §"Journal rotation" (doctor warns at journal > 5 MB);
    25	// §"Filesystem support boundary" (doctor detects + warns on network/shared
    26	// mounts); §CLI contract (--strict exit).
    27	//
    28	// PINS (where the spec left the command surface open — the implementer conforms):
    29	//   D1. SIGNATURE: cmdDoctor(args, io) -> Promise<number>. --json emits exactly
    30	//       one compact {ok,data,warnings,error} envelope on stdout.
    31	//   D2. --json data carries CHECKS and per-platform PROBE RECORDS. Shape is read
    32	//       tolerantly (array of {id, ok, detail?} OR a map for checks; array of
    33	//       {platform, capability, outcome, ...} OR a map for platforms), so the
    34	//       exact container is not over-pinned; what IS pinned:
    35	//         - a check whose id matches /attribution/ reflects the .claude/
    36	//           settings.json attribution state (ok when commit==="" && pr==="");
    37	//         - a check whose id matches /guard|commit|trailer/ is the AI-trailer
    38	//           commit scan;
    39	//         - a check whose id matches /journal/ warns when the journal > 5 MB;
    40	//         - a check whose id matches /filesystem|network|mount|fs/ exists;
    41	//         - EVERY platform record exposes BOTH dimensions: `capability` ∈
    42	//           {installed, authenticated, reachable} (the ordered cumulative
    43	//           ladder; verifier fold F5) and `outcome` ∈ {ok, rate-limited, error,
    44	//           timeout}. A not-installed platform may fall below the ladder; every
    45	//           INSTALLED platform's capability must be one of the three rungs.
    46	//   D3. PROBES ARE TIME-BOUNDED: every probe (non-git) execFile call is invoked
    47	//       with an options object carrying timeout <= 3000 (the hard 3 s bound),
    48	//       mirroring git/snapshot.mjs's timeout:5000 convention.
    49	//   D4. PROBES ARE CACHED ~15 min: a SECOND doctor run within the window makes
    50	//       ZERO new probe (non-git) execFile calls (cache reuse via io.fs), while a
    51	//       run AFTER the window re-probes. (The cache lives under io.fs so it
    52	//       persists across calls; its exact path is left to the implementer.)
    53	//   D5. GIT-GUARD is WORD-BOUNDARY-safe: a commit carrying a "Co-Authored-By:
    54	//       Claude" trailer is flagged (its sha surfaces in the envelope); a history
    55	//       whose only "Claude"-ish token is the name "Claudette Smith" is NOT
    56	//       flagged. doctor scans FULL commit messages (subject + body), where
    57	//       trailers live, and (verifier fold F6) the scan is BOUNDED to the last 50
    58	//       commits — the git log invocation must carry `-n 50` / `-n50` /
    59	//       `--max-count=50` / `--max-count 50` (any of these forms).
    60	//   D6. --strict EXIT: without --strict doctor is report-only (exit 0 even with
    61	//       findings); with --strict a failing check (e.g. a flagged AI trailer)
    62	//       exits non-zero (1). An all-green machine exits 0 either way.
    63	//   D7. PROBE STAGING (verifier fold F5 — the fixture oracle, pinned here since
    64	//       the plan names the dimensions but not the probe wire protocol):
    65	//       doctor probes each platform by invoking its CLI binary noninteractively
    66	//       via io.execFile — `claude` for claude-code, `codex` for codex,
    67	//       `cursor-agent` for cursor (the local CLIs named in docs/design/
    68	//       platform-notes.md; multiple calls per binary are permitted, all answered
    69	//       identically by the fixture). The probe result is classified from the
    70	//       invocation result:
    71	//         - clean resolution                        -> capability 'reachable', outcome 'ok'
    72	//         - output matching /not logged in|log ?in first|unauthorized|authenticat/i
    73	//                                                   -> capability 'installed' (auth
    74	//                                                      unverifiable), outcome != 'ok'
    75	//         - output matching the platform's limit signatures (signatures.v1.json,
    76	//           e.g. "You've hit your usage limit")     -> outcome 'rate-limited'
    77	//           (a server-issued limit response also PROVES auth: capability is at
    78	//           least 'authenticated')
    79	//         - output with auth evidence (/authenticated as|logged in as/i) plus a
    80	//           network failure (/unreachable|ECONN|offline|network/i)
    81	//                                                   -> capability 'authenticated',
    82	//                                                      outcome 'error'
    83	//         - a rejection shaped like an execFile timeout kill (err.killed === true)
    84	//                                                   -> outcome 'timeout'
    85	//         - any other rejection                     -> outcome 'error'
    86	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87	
    88	const ROOT = '/repo';
    89	const paths = bundlePaths(ROOT);
    90	const NOW = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
    91	const plus = (base, ms) => new Date(Date.parse(base) + ms).toISOString();
    92	
    93	const ATTR_OK = JSON.stringify({ attribution: { commit: '', pr: '' } }, null, 2);
    94	
    95	// A git log whose parser sees full commit messages. One commit carries a real
    96	// AI co-author trailer (its sha must be flagged); the rest are clean.
    97	const GIT_LOG_DIRTY =
    98	  'a1b2c3d4 Implement the parser\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\n' +
    99	  'c4d5e6f7 Refactor utils\n\nA routine change with no trailer.\n';
   100	
   101	// Word-boundary control: "Claudette Smith" contains "Claude" as a prefix but is
   102	// NOT an AI-attribution trailer — a \bClaude\b-style scan must NOT flag it.
   103	const GIT_LOG_CLAUDETTE =
   104	  'b2c3d4e5 Add greeting helper\n\nThanks to Claudette Smith for the bug report.\n\n' +
   105	  'e5f6a7b8 Tidy tests\n\nNo attribution here.\n';
   106	
   107	const GIT_LOG_CLEAN = 'f0f0f0f0 Initial commit\n\nClean history.\n';
   108	
   109	// The pinned platform -> probe binary mapping (D7).
   110	const BIN_FOR = { 'claude-code': 'claude', codex: 'codex', cursor: 'cursor-agent' };
   111	
   112	/** Answer one probe invocation according to its staged behavior kind (D7). */
   113	function respondProbe(kind, resolve, reject) {
   114	  switch (kind) {
   115	    case 'ok':
   116	      return resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   117	    case 'auth-fail': {
   118	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   119	      e.code = 1;
   120	      e.stdout = '';
   121	      e.stderr = 'Error: not logged in — run the login flow first\n';
   122	      return reject(e);
   123	    }
   124	    case 'net-fail': {
   125	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   126	      e.code = 1;
   127	      e.stdout = '';
   128	      e.stderr = 'Authenticated as user@example.test, but the service is unreachable (ECONNREFUSED)\n';
   129	      return reject(e);
   130	    }
   131	    case 'rate-limited': {
   132	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   133	      e.code = 1;
   134	      e.stdout = '';
   135	      e.stderr = "You've hit your usage limit. Please try again at 3pm.\n";
   136	      return reject(e);
   137	    }
   138	    case 'timeout': {
   139	      const e = new Error('timed out');
   140	      e.killed = true;
   141	      e.signal = 'SIGTERM';
   142	      return reject(e);
   143	    }
   144	    case 'error': {
   145	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   146	      e.code = 1;
   147	      e.stdout = '';
   148	      e.stderr = 'fatal: unexpected internal error\n';
   149	      return reject(e);
   150	    }
   151	    default:
   152	      return resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   153	  }
   154	}
   155	
   156	/**
   157	 * Build a doctor io: fakeio for fs/stdout, but execFile REPLACED with a
   158	 * responder that answers git subcommands from `gitLog`/clean fixtures and
   159	 * treats every non-git command as a platform probe answered per `probes`
   160	 * (a map of binary basename -> behavior kind; default all 'ok').
   161	 * io.__calls records {cmd, args, opts}.
   162	 */
   163	function makeDoctorIo({ files = {}, gitLog = GIT_LOG_CLEAN, now = NOW, probes = {} } = {}) {
   164	  const io = makeIo({ files, env: { HOME: '/home/user' }, now });
   165	  const calls = [];
   166	  io.__calls = calls;
   167	  io.execFile = (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => {
   168	    calls.push({ cmd, args: [...args], opts });
   169	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
   170	      if (cmd === 'git') {
   171	        const sub = args[0];
   172	        if (sub === 'log') return resolve({ stdout: gitLog, stderr: '' });
   173	        if (sub === 'rev-parse') return resolve({ stdout: args.includes('HEAD') && !args.includes('--abbrev-ref') ? 'f0f0f0f0\n' : 'main\n', stderr: '' });
   174	        return resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   175	      }
   176	      // Non-git command === a platform probe; behavior keyed on the binary basename.
   177	      const bin = String(cmd).split('/').pop();
   178	      return respondProbe(probes[bin] ?? 'ok', resolve, reject);
   179	    });
   180	  };
   181	  return io;
   182	}
   183	
   184	const probeCalls = (io) => io.__calls.filter((c) => c.cmd !== 'git');
   185	
   186	/** Parse the single compact --json envelope and check its wire shape. */
   187	function envelope(io) {
   188	  const parsed = JSON.parse(io.stdoutText());
   189	  assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), JSON.stringify(parsed) + '\n', 'stdout is exactly one compact envelope + newline');
   190	  assert.ok('ok' in parsed && 'data' in parsed && 'warnings' in parsed && 'error' in parsed, 'envelope carries ok/data/warnings/error');
   191	  return parsed;
   192	}
   193	
   194	/** Normalize data.checks (array of {id,ok} OR a map) to an array of {id, ok, ...}. */
   195	function checkList(env) {
   196	  const c = env.data && env.data.checks;
   197	  if (Array.isArray(c)) return c;
   198	  if (c && typeof c === 'object') return Object.entries(c).map(([id, v]) => ({ id, ...(v && typeof v === 'object' ? v : { ok: v }) }));
   199	  return [];
   200	}
   201	const findCheck = (env, re) => checkList(env).find((c) => re.test(String(c.id)));
   202	
   203	/** Normalize data.platforms (array OR map) to an array of records. */
   204	function platformRecords(env) {
   205	  const p = env.data && env.data.platforms;
   206	  if (Array.isArray(p)) return p;
   207	  if (p && typeof p === 'object') return Object.entries(p).map(([platform, v]) => ({ platform, ...(v && typeof v === 'object' ? v : {}) }));
   208	  return [];
   209	}
   210	const recordFor = (env, platform) => platformRecords(env).find((r) => r.platform === platform);
   211	
   212	const LADDER = ['installed', 'authenticated', 'reachable'];
   213	const OUTCOMES = ['ok', 'rate-limited', 'error', 'timeout'];
   214	
   215	const ATTR_FILES = { [`${ROOT}/.claude/settings.json`]: ATTR_OK, ['/home/user/.claude/settings.json']: ATTR_OK };
   216	
   217	// ===========================================================================
   218	describe('doctor — envelope + check aggregation', () => {
   219	  it('--json emits one compact envelope carrying checks and per-platform probe records', async () => {
   220	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES } });
   221	    const code = await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   222	    assert.equal(code, 0, `an all-green machine exits 0; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
   223	
   224	    const env = envelope(io);
   225	    assert.ok(checkList(env).length > 0, 'doctor reports a set of checks');
   226	
   227	    assert.ok(findCheck(env, /attribution/), 'an attribution-settings check is present');
   228	    assert.ok(findCheck(env, /guard|commit|trailer/), 'a git-guard commit-scan check is present');
   229	    assert.ok(findCheck(env, /journal/), 'a journal-size check is present');
   230	    assert.ok(findCheck(env, /filesystem|network|mount|fs/), 'a filesystem check is present');
   231	  });
   232	});
   233	
   234	// ===========================================================================
   235	describe('doctor — per-platform probe records expose BOTH dimensions (F5)', () => {
   236	  it('with every probe healthy: outcome is "ok"; capability reflects what each probe PROVES (gate-2 iter-2 M3)', async () => {
   237	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES } });
   238	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   239	    const records = platformRecords(envelope(io));
   240	    assert.ok(records.length >= 3, 'the three platforms each get a probe record');
   241	    for (const r of records) {
   242	      assert.ok(LADDER.includes(r.capability), `${r.platform}: capability must be a ladder rung (${LADDER.join('→')}); got ${JSON.stringify(r.capability)}`);
   243	      assert.ok(OUTCOMES.includes(r.outcome), `${r.platform}: outcome must be one of ${OUTCOMES.join('|')}; got ${JSON.stringify(r.outcome)}`);
   244	      assert.equal(r.outcome, 'ok', `${r.platform}: a clean probe outcome is ok`);
   245	    }
   246	    const cap = Object.fromEntries(records.map((/** @type {any} */ r) => [r.platform, r.capability]));
   247	    // claude-code's probe is `claude --version` — a clean exit proves only that
   248	    // the binary is INSTALLED, not that the server was reached or auth is valid.
   249	    assert.equal(cap['claude-code'], 'installed', 'a --version probe proves installed, never reachable/authenticated');
   250	    // codex/cursor probes are auth-checking status commands → a clean exit reaches.
   251	    assert.equal(cap.codex, 'reachable', 'a clean auth-status probe verifies the full ladder');
   252	    assert.equal(cap.cursor, 'reachable', 'a clean auth-status probe verifies the full ladder');
   253	  });
   254	
   255	  it('probe fixtures A: installed-only (auth failure), rate-limited (proves auth), reachable (clean)', async () => {
   256	    const io = makeDoctorIo({
   257	      files: { ...ATTR_FILES },
   258	      probes: { [BIN_FOR['claude-code']]: 'auth-fail', [BIN_FOR.codex]: 'rate-limited', [BIN_FOR.cursor]: 'ok' },
   259	    });
   260	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   261	    const env = envelope(io);
   262	
   263	    const cc = recordFor(env, 'claude-code');
   264	    assert.equal(cc.capability, 'installed', 'an auth failure stops the ladder at installed (auth unverifiable)');
   265	    assert.notEqual(cc.outcome, 'ok', 'an auth-failed probe is not outcome ok');
   266	    assert.ok(OUTCOMES.includes(cc.outcome));
   267	
   268	    const cx = recordFor(env, 'codex');
   269	    assert.equal(cx.outcome, 'rate-limited', 'a server-issued limit response records outcome rate-limited');
   270	    assert.ok(['authenticated', 'reachable'].includes(cx.capability), 'a rate-limit response proves at least authenticated');
   271	
   272	    const cu = recordFor(env, 'cursor');
   273	    assert.equal(cu.capability, 'reachable', 'a clean probe verifies reachable');
   274	    assert.equal(cu.outcome, 'ok');
   275	  });
   276	
   277	  it('probe fixtures B: authenticated-but-unreachable, timeout, error', async () => {
   278	    const io = makeDoctorIo({
   279	      files: { ...ATTR_FILES },
   280	      probes: { [BIN_FOR['claude-code']]: 'net-fail', [BIN_FOR.codex]: 'timeout', [BIN_FOR.cursor]: 'error' },
     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';

    48	describe('M2 — Windows path handling', () => {
    49	  it('resolveRoot ascends a backslash path to the marker directory', () => {
    50	    const io = makeIo({ files: { 'C:/repo/baton.config.json': '{}', 'C:/repo/sub/deep/x.txt': 'x' } });
    51	    io.cwd = 'C:\\repo\\sub\\deep';
    52	    // Node fs accepts forward slashes on Windows, so the fake stores C:/repo/…;
    53	    // the ascent must still recognize the backslash-separated cwd.
    54	    assert.equal(resolveRoot(io, {}), 'C:/repo');
    55	  });
    56	
    57	  it('jailRelPath rejects a Windows-absolute path and normalizes backslashes', () => {
    58	    assert.equal(jailRelPath('C:\\Windows\\system32\\x'), null);
    59	    assert.equal(jailRelPath('src\\a\\b.js'), 'src/a/b.js');
    60	  });
    61	
    62	  // checkHandoffTree's realpath containment compares dirReal against
    63	  // `${rootReal}/.handoff`. On Windows realpathSync returns BACKSLASH paths, so
    64	  // an un-normalized compare mismatches on every managed tree and refuses ALL
    65	  // operations. The memfs fake can't model drive-letter roots (it registers
    66	  // dirs as '/'-absolute), so use a precise hand-built io.fs that returns the
    67	  // Windows realpath spellings for the two paths checkHandoffTree resolves.
    68	  const DIR = 'C:/repo/.handoff';
    69	  const winIo = (rootReal, dirReal) => ({
    70	    fs: {
    71	      existsSync: (/** @type {any} */ p) => String(p) === DIR,
    72	      lstatSync: (/** @type {any} */ p) => ({
    73	        isSymbolicLink: () => false,
    74	        isDirectory: () => String(p) === DIR,
    75	        isFile: () => String(p) !== DIR,
    76	      }),
    77	      realpathSync: (/** @type {any} */ p) => {
    78	        const s = String(p);
    79	        if (s === 'C:/repo') return rootReal;
    80	        if (s === DIR) return dirReal;
    81	        return s;
    82	      },
    83	      readdirSync: () => [], // empty tree: the walk finds nothing to refuse
    84	    },
    85	  });
    86	
    87	  it('checkHandoffTree ACCEPTS an in-tree .handoff whose realpath is backslash-spelled', () => {
    88	    const io = winIo('C:\\repo', 'C:\\repo\\.handoff');
    89	    assert.deepEqual(checkHandoffTree('C:/repo', io), { ok: true });
    90	  });
    91	
    92	  it('checkHandoffTree REFUSES a .handoff realpath outside the root (backslash)', () => {
    93	    const io = winIo('C:\\repo', 'C:\\elsewhere\\.handoff');
    94	    const r = checkHandoffTree('C:/repo', io);
    95	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
    96	    assert.match(r.problem, /outside the repository root/i);
    97	  });
    98	
    99	  it('checkHandoffTree REFUSES a cross-volume .handoff realpath', () => {
   100	    const io = winIo('C:\\repo', 'D:\\repo\\.handoff');
   101	    const r = checkHandoffTree('C:/repo', io);
   102	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   103	    assert.match(r.problem, /outside the repository root/i);
   104	  });
   105	});
   106	
   107	describe('M3 — doctor probe dimensions', () => {
   108	  const baseExec = () => ({ 'git log -n 50 --format=%h %s%n%n%b': { stdout: 'abc clean\n\n\n' } });
   109	  const probeCall = (io) => JSON.parse(io.stdoutText()).data.platforms;
   110

   119	  });

   121	  it('remap with NO probe cache flags offline selections degraded (plan: offline always degraded)', () => {

   129

   137	    assert.ok(

   139	      'a fully-probed platform resolves without a degraded flag',
   140	    );
   141	  });

   144	describe('M4 — low-confidence usage-limit does not silently avoid', () => {
   145	  // The origin is cursor, and this role puts cursor FIRST-eligible. Avoidance is
   146	  // therefore OBSERVABLE: not avoiding cursor means the role resolves to cursor;
   147	  // avoiding it makes the role fall through to claude-code. (The template's
   148	  // implementer chain has cursor LAST, so claude-code wins regardless — the
   149	  // earlier "not avoided" assertion could not fail, i.e. it was vacuous.)
   150	  const CURSOR_FIRST_CONFIG = JSON.stringify({
   151	    schema: 'baton/config@1',
   152	    roles: { cursorFirst: ['cursor/composer', 'claude-code/claude-fable-5'] },
   153	    platforms: { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} },
   154	    defaults: { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' },
   155	  });
   156	  const avoidedCursor = (/** @type {any} */ role) =>
   157	    (role.skipped ?? []).some((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === 'cursor' && s.why === 'avoided');
   158	
   159	  const sealed = (reasonClass) => ({
   160	    schema: 'baton/bundle@1',
   161	    bundleId: 'b_conf0000000000',
   162	    generation: 1,
   163	    createdAt: T0,
   164	    updatedAt: T0,
   165	    origin: { platform: 'cursor', model: 'composer', sessionHint: 's', unstable: false },
   166	    task: { goal: 'g', constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
   167	    plan: { steps: [] },
   168	    decisions: [],
   169	    files: { touched: [] },
   170	    roles: { assignments: {} },
   171	    git: null,
   172	    // Unsealed (open) so classifyReason runs on the reason text, not a seal.
   173	    handoff: { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] },
   174	    journalSeq: 0,
   175	    compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
   176	    dedupeRing: [],
   177	  });
   178	
   179	  function prep(reason, extraOpts = {}) {
   180	    const io = makeIo({
   181	      files: { '/repo/baton.config.json': CURSOR_FIRST_CONFIG, [SIG_PATH]: SIG_TABLE, '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': JSON.stringify(sealed(null), null, 2) + '\n' },
   182	      now: T0,
   183	    });
   184	    return prepare('/repo', { platform: 'claude-code', origin: 'cursor', reason, probes: null, sessionHint: 'cli', ...extraOpts }, io);
   185	  }
   186	
   187	  it('a low-confidence "quota exceeded" reason warns and KEEPS cursor selected (first-eligible)', () => {
   188	    const { assignments, warnings } = prep('quota exceeded');
   189	    assert.ok(warnings.some((w) => /low confidence|heuristic|--reason-class/i.test(w)), `a low-confidence warning is surfaced: ${JSON.stringify(warnings)}`);
   190	    // Not avoiding cursor is observable: the first-eligible role resolves TO it.
   191	    assert.equal(assignments.cursorFirst.platform, 'cursor', 'a low-confidence heuristic must not silently avoid the origin — cursor stays selected');
   192	    assert.equal(avoidedCursor(assignments.cursorFirst), false, 'cursor is not skipped-as-avoided');
   193	  });
   194	
   195	  it('an explicit --reason-class usage-limit DOES avoid cursor (role falls through to claude-code)', () => {
   196	    const { assignments } = prep('quota exceeded', { reasonClass: 'usage-limit' });
   197	    assert.equal(assignments.cursorFirst.platform, 'claude-code', 'explicit confirmation avoids cursor — the role falls through');
   198	    assert.equal(avoidedCursor(assignments.cursorFirst), true, 'cursor is recorded as avoided');
   199	  });
   200	
   201	  it('a HIGH-confidence (sealed reasonClass) usage-limit avoids cursor without confirmation', () => {
   202	    // A sealed usage-limit reasonClass is a trusted, high-confidence classification.
   203	    const io = makeIo({
   204	      files: { '/repo/baton.config.json': CURSOR_FIRST_CONFIG, [SIG_PATH]: SIG_TABLE, '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': JSON.stringify(sealed('usage-limit'), null, 2) + '\n' },
   205	      now: T0,
   206	    });
   207	    const { assignments } = prepare('/repo', { platform: 'claude-code', origin: 'cursor', reason: 'sealed reason', probes: null, sessionHint: 'cli' }, io);
   208	    assert.equal(assignments.cursorFirst.platform, 'claude-code', 'a sealed usage-limit reasonClass avoids cursor');
   209	    assert.equal(avoidedCursor(assignments.cursorFirst), true, 'cursor is recorded as avoided');
   210	  });
   211	});
   300	        schema: 'baton/config@1',
   301	        roles: { r: [e('codex', 'a')] },
   302	        platforms: { 'claude-code': {}, codex: { enabled: true }, cursor: {} },
   303	        defaults: { ...DEFAULTS },
   304	      },
   305	      to: 'codex',
   306	    });
   307	    assert.equal(assignments.r.mode, 'native');
   308	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'codex');
   309	    assert.equal(assignments.r.skipped.length, 0);
   310	  });
   311	});
   312	
   313	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   314	describe('resolveRoles — probe eligibility & degraded flag', () => {

   324	  });
   325

   334

   343	    assert.equal(assignments.r.degraded, true);

   345	
   346	  it('probes === null (offline) resolves eligible but flagged degraded (gate-2 iter-2 M3; plan: offline always degraded)', () => {
   347	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   348	      config: oneRole([e('codex', 'a')]),
   349	      to: 'codex',

   351	    });

     1	import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
     2	import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
     3	import { loadBundle, writeSnapshotIn, rotateJournalIn } from '../bundle/store.mjs';
     4	import { withLock } from '../bundle/lock.mjs';

     8	import { loadSignatures } from '../detect/signatures.mjs';
     9	import { classify } from '../detect/classifier.mjs';
    10	import { dedupeKey } from '../util/ids.mjs';
    11	
    12	const BUILTIN_SIGNATURES = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'data', 'signatures.v1.json');
    13	const STALE_AFTER_MS = 12 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
    14	
    15	/** @typedef {{platform: string, origin: string, reason: string, gitSnapshot: any, probes?: any, sessionHint: string, sessionUnstable?: boolean, reasonClass?: string}} ReceiveOpts */
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * Classify the switch reason, KEEPING confidence (gate-2 iter-2 M4): a
    19	 * low-confidence heuristic (e.g. Cursor's "quota exceeded") must not silently
    20	 * drive an avoid[] decision — the caller warns and defers to explicit intake.
    21	 * @param {string} reason @param {string} originPlatform @param {any} io
    22	 * @returns {{class: string | null, confidence: string | null}}
    23	 */
    24	function classifyReason(reason, originPlatform, io) {
    25	  try {
    26	    const table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, io);
    27	    const verdict = classify({ text: reason, exitCode: 0, platform: originPlatform, table, structured: null });
    28	    return verdict.class === 'ok' ? { class: null, confidence: null } : { class: verdict.class, confidence: verdict.confidence };
    29	  } catch {
    30	    return { class: null, confidence: null };
    31	  }
    32	}
    33	
    34	/**
    35	 * The revision fingerprint the receipt token is a hash of. Every field is a
    36	 * bound input: any drift between prepare and commit re-derives a different
    37	 * token, so a stale receipt can never commit (plan §Concurrency).
    38	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
    39	 */
    40	function deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io) {
    41	  let configDigest = 'absent';
    42	  try {
    43	    configDigest = dedupeKey(io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/baton.config.json`, 'utf8'));
    44	  } catch {
    45	    // no config — 'absent' is itself a bound value
    46	  }
    47	  return dedupeKey({
    48	    generation: bundle.generation,
    49	    journalSeq: bundle.journalSeq,
    50	    // The seal state is a bound input too (gate-2 fix): a competing receiver
    51	    // that lands between this token's derivation and its commit changes
    52	    // status/receive_log, so the loser's token cannot re-derive.
    53	    handoffStatus: bundle.handoff?.status ?? null,
    54	    receives: Array.isArray(bundle.handoff?.receive_log) ? bundle.handoff.receive_log.length : 0,
    55	    origin: opts.origin,
    56	    reason: opts.reason,
    57	    // Explicit --reason-class is a bound intake input (gate-2 iter-2 M4): it
    58	    // changes the avoid[] decision, so a change between prepare and commit is
    59	    // drift that must re-prepare.
    60	    reasonClass: opts.reasonClass ?? null,
    61	    configDigest,
    62	    gitDigest: dedupeKey(opts.gitSnapshot ?? null),
    63	    probesDigest: dedupeKey(opts.probes ?? null),
    64	    platform: opts.platform,
    65	    sessionHint: opts.sessionHint,
    66	  });
    67	}
    68	
    69	/**
    70	 * @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
    71	 * @returns {string[]} staleness/degradation warnings
    72	 */
    73	function stalenessWarnings(bundle, opts, io) {
    74	  /** @type {string[]} */
    75	  const warnings = [];
    76	  if (bundle.handoff.status === 'open') {
    77	    warnings.push('source bundle is unsealed (open) — the handoff was never finalized; treating this as a degraded (limit-death) receive');
    78	  }
    79	  const ref = bundle.handoff.finalizedAt ?? bundle.updatedAt;
    80	  const ageMs = Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(ref);
    81	  if (Number.isFinite(ageMs) && ageMs > STALE_AFTER_MS) {
    82	    warnings.push(`bundle finalized ${Math.round(ageMs / 3_600_000)}h ago (staleness threshold 12h) — re-verify before continuing`);
    83	  }
    84	  const g = opts.gitSnapshot;
    85	  if (g && bundle.git) {
    86	    if (bundle.git.headSha !== g.headSha) {
    87	      warnings.push(`HEAD moved since capture: bundle recorded ${bundle.git.headSha}, working tree now ${g.headSha}`);
    88	    }
    89	    if (bundle.git.dirty !== g.dirty) {
    90	      warnings.push(`dirty-state mismatch: bundle captured dirty=${bundle.git.dirty}, working tree now dirty=${g.dirty}`);
    91	    }
    92	  }
    93	  return warnings;
    94	}
    95	
    96	/**
    97	 * Phase 1 of the receive transaction: validate, resolve roles, render the
    98	 * resume prompt, and return a one-time receipt token. Mutates NOTHING — the
    99	 * token is self-contained (a fingerprint hash), never a persisted record.
   100	 * @param {string} root @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   101	 * @returns {{token: string, prompt: string, assignments: Record<string, any>, warnings: string[]}}
   102	 */
   103	export function prepare(root, opts, io) {
   104	  const { bundle, warnings: loadWarnings } = loadBundle(root, io);
   105	  if (bundle === null) throw new Error(`no handoff bundle under ${root}/.handoff — nothing to receive`);
   106	
   107	  const warnings = [...loadWarnings, ...stalenessWarnings(bundle, opts, io)];
   108	
   109	  const { config, errors } = loadConfig(root, io);
   110	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
   111	  let assignments = {};
   112	  if (config) {
   113	    // The dead origin comes from the BUNDLE first (its sealed reasonClass and
   114	    // recorded origin platform); typed intake only fills the gaps — a user
   115	    // should not need to retype the verbatim limit string to keep roles off
   116	    // the platform that died (gate-2 reviewer-b finding 4). Confidence gates
   117	    // auto-avoidance (gate-2 iter-2 M4): an explicit --reason-class or a sealed
   118	    // reasonClass is trusted; a live text classification only auto-avoids at
   119	    // medium+ confidence, else it warns and defers to explicit intake.
   120	    let reasonClass;
   121	    let confidence;
   122	    if (typeof opts.reasonClass === 'string') {
   123	      reasonClass = opts.reasonClass;
   124	      confidence = 'high'; // explicit user intake
   125	    } else if (typeof bundle.handoff?.reasonClass === 'string') {
   126	      reasonClass = bundle.handoff.reasonClass;
   127	      confidence = 'high'; // trusted seal
   128	    } else {
   129	      const v = classifyReason(opts.reason, opts.origin, io);
   130	      reasonClass = v.class;
   131	      confidence = v.confidence;
   132	    }
   133	    const deadOrigin =
   134	      typeof bundle.origin?.platform === 'string' && bundle.origin.platform !== 'unknown' ? bundle.origin.platform : opts.origin;
   135	    /** @type {string[]} */
   136	    let avoid = [];
   137	    if (reasonClass === 'usage-limit') {
   138	      if (confidence === 'low') {
   139	        warnings.push(
   140	          `the switch reason matched a usage-limit heuristic only at LOW confidence — not auto-avoiding ${deadOrigin}; pass --reason-class usage-limit to confirm the failover away from it`,
   141	        );
   142	      } else {
   143	        avoid = [deadOrigin];
   144	      }
   145	    }
   146	    assignments = resolveRoles({ config, to: opts.platform, avoid, nativeOnly: false, probes: opts.probes ?? null }).assignments;
   147	  } else {
   148	    warnings.push(`role matrix unavailable (${errors.map((e) => e.msg).join('; ')}) — no role table in this prompt`);
   149	  }
   150	
   151	  const prompt = renderResumePrompt({ bundle, assignments, warnings, origin: opts.origin, reason: opts.reason });
   152	  return { token: deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io), prompt, assignments, warnings };
   153	}
   154	
   155	/**
   156	 * Phase 2: one atomic transition. Re-derives every bound input from current
   157	 * disk state + opts and rejects ANY drift (throws, zero mutation). On match:
   158	 * writes the received seal, archives it via a 'receive' rotation, then opens a
   159	 * fresh writable generation owned by the receiving platform/session.
   160	 * @param {string} root @param {string} token @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   161	 * @returns {{adopted: boolean, generation: number, archivedTo: string}}
   162	 */
   163	export function commit(root, token, opts, io) {
   164	  // ONE operation-scoped lock across revalidation and every mutation (gate-2
   165	  // fix: reviewers a#1/b#11): two competing receivers serialize here, and the
   166	  // loser's re-derivation sees the winner's generation/status/receive_log.
   167	  return withLock(root, io, (fence) => commitLocked(root, token, opts, io, fence));
   168	}
   169	
   170	/**
   171	 * @param {string} root @param {string} token @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io @param {string} fence
   172	 * @returns {{adopted: boolean, generation: number, archivedTo: string}}
   173	 */
   174	function commitLocked(root, token, opts, io, fence) {
   175	  const { bundle } = loadBundle(root, io);
   176	  if (bundle === null) throw new Error(`no handoff bundle under ${root}/.handoff — nothing to receive; run receive --prepare first`);
   177	
   178	  if (deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io) !== token) {
   179	    throw new Error(
   180	      'receipt token is stale: a bound input (bundle revision, intake, config, git state, probes, or target platform/session) drifted since prepare — run re-prepare and retry',
   181	    );
   182	  }
   183	
   184	  const degraded = bundle.handoff.status !== 'sealed';
   185	  const receiveEntry = {
   186	    origin: opts.origin,
   187	    reason: opts.reason,
   188	    at: io.now(),
   189	    ...(degraded ? { degradedSeal: true } : {}),
   190	  };
   191	
   192	  const receivedSeal = {
   193	    ...bundle,
   194	    updatedAt: io.now(),
   195	    handoff: {
   196	      ...bundle.handoff,
   197	      status: 'received',
   198	      ...(degraded
   199	        ? {
   200	            reason: opts.reason,
   201	            // Explicit intake wins; else the live text classification's class.
   202	            reasonClass: typeof opts.reasonClass === 'string' ? opts.reasonClass : classifyReason(opts.reason, opts.origin, io).class,
   203	            finalizedAt: io.now(),
   204	          }
   205	        : {}),
   206	      receive_log: [...bundle.handoff.receive_log, receiveEntry],
   207	    },
   208	  };
   209	
   210	  // Freeze order: the received seal is written first so the 'receive' rotation
   211	  // archives exactly that state (never the pre-commit bundle). All three
   212	  // mutations run under the SAME lock acquisition, fence-checked per write.
   213	  writeSnapshotIn(root, receivedSeal, io, fence);
   214	  const archivedTo = rotateJournalIn(root, 'receive', io, fence);
   215	
   216	  const { config } = loadConfig(root, io);
   217	  const next = {
   218	    ...receivedSeal,
   219	    generation: bundle.generation + 1,
   220	    origin: {
   221	      platform: opts.platform,
   222	      model: config?.defaults?.[opts.platform] ?? 'unknown',
   223	      sessionHint: opts.sessionHint,
   224	      unstable: opts.sessionUnstable === true,
   225	    },
   226	    handoff: { ...receivedSeal.handoff, status: 'open' },
   227	  };
   228	  writeSnapshotIn(root, next, io, fence);
   229	
   230	  return { adopted: true, generation: next.generation, archivedTo };
   231	}
     1	import { snapshot as gitSnapshot } from '../git/snapshot.mjs';
     2	import { prepare, commit } from '../receive/txn.mjs';
     3	import { readProbeCache } from '../roles/availability.mjs';
     4	import { emitEnvelope, parseFlags, resolveRoot, usageError } from './shared.mjs';
     5	
     6	/**
     7	 * `baton receive` — the two-phase resume flow. `--print-prompt` and
     8	 * `--prepare` are read-only (prepare mutates nothing); `--commit <token>`
     9	 * performs the atomic transition. The receiving session hint defaults to a
    10	 * host-stable value so prepare and commit issued by separate CLI processes
    11	 * still derive the same token; adapters pass `--session` with their real id.
    12	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    13	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    14	 */
    15	export async function cmdReceive(args, io) {
    16	  const { flags } = parseFlags(args);
    17	  const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
    18	  if (!platform) return usageError(io, flags, 'receive', '--platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required');
    19	
    20	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    21	  const git = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
    22	  const hasSession = typeof flags.session === 'string';
    23	  const opts = {
    24	    platform,
    25	    origin: typeof flags.origin === 'string' ? flags.origin : 'unknown',
    26	    reason: typeof flags.reason === 'string' ? flags.reason : 'unspecified',
    27	    gitSnapshot: git,
    28	    // The fresh probe cache is a bound token input (probesDigest): remap uses
    29	    // it at prepare, and a cache change before commit is drift → re-prepare.
    30	    probes: readProbeCache(root, io),
    31	    // Explicit reason-class intake (gate-2 iter-2 M4): confirms a failover away
    32	    // from a low-confidence-classified origin; bound into the token.
    33	    ...(typeof flags['reason-class'] === 'string' ? { reasonClass: flags['reason-class'] } : {}),
    34	    sessionHint: hasSession ? /** @type {string} */ (flags.session) : `cli:${io.host}`,
    35	    // A defaulted host-derived hint is NOT the harness session id — mark it
    36	    // unstable so the receiving session's first real hook checkpoint adopts
    37	    // ownership instead of being rejected as foreign (gate-2 fix 2).
    38	    sessionUnstable: !hasSession,
    39	  };
    40	
    41	  if (typeof flags.commit === 'string') {
    42	    try {
    43	      const res = commit(root, flags.commit, opts, io);
    44	      if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: res });
    45	      else io.stdout.write(`received — generation ${res.generation} open on ${platform}; archived ${res.archivedTo}\n`);
    46	      return 0;
    47	    } catch (err) {
    48	      const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
    49	      if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'commit-rejected', msg } });
    50	      else io.stderr.write(`baton receive: ${msg}\n`);
    51	      return 1;
    52	    }
    53	  }
    54	
    55	  /** @type {ReturnType<typeof prepare>} */
    56	  let prepared;
    57	  try {
    58	    prepared = prepare(root, opts, io);
    59	  } catch (err) {
    60	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
    61	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'prepare-failed', msg } });
    62	    else io.stderr.write(`baton receive: ${msg}\n`);
    63	    return 1;
    64	  }
    65	
    66	  if (flags['print-prompt'] === true) {
    67	    // --json wraps the prompt in the envelope (gate-2 iter-2 M6); the bare
    68	    // form stays raw markdown — the adapter hook fallback path is unchanged.
    69	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { prompt: prepared.prompt, warnings: prepared.warnings } });
    70	    else io.stdout.write(prepared.prompt);
    71	    return 0;
    72	  }
    73	
    74	  if (flags.json) {
    75	    emitEnvelope(io, {
    76	      ok: true,
    77	      data: { token: prepared.token, prompt: prepared.prompt, assignments: prepared.assignments, warnings: prepared.warnings },
    78	    });
    79	  } else {
    80	    for (const w of prepared.warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton receive: ${w}\n`);
    81	    io.stderr.write(`baton receive: token ${prepared.token}\n`);
    82	    io.stdout.write(prepared.prompt);
    83	  }
    84	  return 0;
    85	}

    33	function version() {
    34	  const pkgPath = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'package.json');
    35	  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8')).version;
    36	}
    37	
    38	const USAGE = `usage: baton <command> [options]
    39	
    40	commands:
    41	  ${COMMANDS.join(', ')}
    42	
    43	global options:
    44	  --version   print version
    45	  --json      machine-readable output: one compact JSON envelope on stdout
    46	`;
    47	
    48	/**
    49	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
    50	 * @param {any} io
    51	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
    52	 */
    53	function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
    54	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    55	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
    56	}
    57	
    58	/**
    59	 * @param {string[]} argv process args after the binary
    60	 * @param {any} io injected {cwd, stdout, stderr}
    61	 * @returns {number | Promise<number>} exit code
    62	 */
    63	export function run(argv, io) {
    64	  const args = argv.filter((a) => a !== '');
    65	  // Global --json intent is parsed BEFORE dispatch (gate-2 iter-2 M6): a
    66	  // top-level `--json` with no command, or --help/--version, must still emit
    67	  // exactly one envelope rather than raw text.
    68	  const jsonWanted = args.includes('--json');
    69	  const cmdIndex = args.findIndex((a) => !a.startsWith('--'));
    70	  const cmd = cmdIndex === -1 ? undefined : args[cmdIndex];
    71	
    72	  if (cmd === undefined) {
    73	    // No command — just flags (or nothing).
    74	    if (args.includes('--version')) {
    75	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { version: version() } });
    76	      else io.stdout.write(version() + '\n');
    77	      return 0;
    78	    }
    79	    if (args.includes('--help')) {
    80	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage: USAGE.trim() } });
    81	      else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
    82	      return 0;
    83	    }
    84	    // Bare invocation (or a lone --json): a usage error.
    85	    if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'no command given' } });
    86	    else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
    87	    return 2;
    88	  }
    89	
    90	  // All flags (before and after the command token) reach the command; only the
    91	  // command token itself is stripped, so a leading `--json` isn't lost.
    92	  const rest = args.filter((_, i) => i !== cmdIndex);
    93	
    94	  // Usage errors honor the --json envelope contract too (gate-2 fix 11 + M6:
    95	  // global --json counts, wherever it sits).
    96	  const wantsJson = jsonWanted;
    97	  if (cmd === 'wrap') {
    98	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1' } });
    99	    else io.stderr.write(`baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1.\n${USAGE}`);
   100	    return 2;
   101	  }
   102	  if (!COMMANDS.includes(cmd)) {
   103	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: `unknown command '${cmd}'` } });
   104	    else io.stderr.write(`baton: unknown command '${cmd}'\n${USAGE}`);
   105	    return 2;
   106	  }
   107	
   108	  if (rest.includes('--help')) {
   109	    io.stdout.write(`usage: baton ${cmd} [options]\n\nSee README for the ${cmd} contract.\n`);
   110	    return 0;
   111	  }
   112	
   113	  if (cmd === 'status') return cmdStatus(rest, io);
   114	  if (cmd === 'detect') return cmdDetect(rest, io);
   115	  if (cmd === 'remap') return cmdRemap(rest, io);
   116	  if (cmd === 'checkpoint') return cmdCheckpoint(rest, io);
   117	  if (cmd === 'finalize') return cmdFinalize(rest, io);
   118	  if (cmd === 'receive') return cmdReceive(rest, io);
   119	  if (cmd === 'init') return cmdInit(rest, io);
   120	  if (cmd === 'doctor') return cmdDoctor(rest, io);
   121	  if (cmd === 'purge-transcript') return cmdPurgeTranscript(rest, io);
   122	  if (cmd === 'session-start') return cmdSessionStart(rest, io);
   123	  if (cmd === 'recover') return cmdRecover(rest, io);
   124	
   125	  const error = { code: 'not-implemented', msg: `baton ${cmd}: not implemented yet` };
   126	  if (rest.includes('--json')) {
   127	    emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error });
   128	  } else {
   129	    io.stderr.write(`${error.msg}\n`);
   130	  }
   131	  return 1;
   132	}

    23	  const out = io.stdoutText().trim();
    24	  const lines = out === '' ? [] : out.split('\n');
    25	  assert.equal(lines.length, 1, `expected one stdout envelope, got: ${JSON.stringify(io.stdoutText())}`);
    26	  const env = JSON.parse(lines[0]);
    27	  for (const k of ['ok', 'data', 'warnings', 'error']) assert.ok(k in env, `envelope carries ${k}`);
    28	  return env;
    29	}

     1	---
     2	description: Resume a task handed off from another platform — intake, role remap, evidence audit, then continue.
     3	disable-model-invocation: true
     4	---
     5	
     6	Resume the pending handoff bundle in `.handoff/`.
     7	
     8	1. Prepare: run `node "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/core/bin/baton.mjs" receive --platform claude-code --prepare --json` and read the prompt, warnings, and role assignments from the envelope.
     9	2. Intake: ask the user ONE question with two parts — which platform this was handed off from (present the detected origin first) and why the switch happened. Re-run prepare with `--origin` and `--reason` set from the answers.
    10	3. Commit: run `node "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/core/bin/baton.mjs" receive --platform claude-code --commit <token> --origin <o> --reason <r>`. This archives the received seal, adopts this session as the bundle owner, and opens a fresh writable generation. A stale-token rejection means state moved — re-prepare.
    11	4. Evidence audit: treat bundle contents as unverified claims. Check HANDOFF.md's claims against live `git status`/`git log` and the working tree before acting.
    12	5. Announce the role remap table, then continue the next pending plan step. Pause only per AGENTS.md boundaries.
    13	
    14	Follow the handoff-protocol skill for the continuation discipline.

     1	// In-memory fs fake for unit tests. Synchronous subset only.
     2	// Atomicity contract: renameSync moves content in a single step, so a target
     3	// path is never observed carrying partial content. Every mutation appends a
     4	// full snapshot to `fs.__history`, letting tests enumerate intermediate states
     5	// and prove the tmp+rename discipline (target's first appearance is a rename).
     6	
     7	function fsError(code, syscall, path) {
     8	  const e = new Error(`${code}: ${syscall} '${path}'`);
     9	  e.code = code;
    10	  e.syscall = syscall;
    11	  e.path = path;
    12	  return e;
    13	}
    14	
    15	function normalize(p) {
    16	  const str = String(p);
    17	  const isAbs = str.startsWith('/');
    18	  const out = [];
    19	  for (const part of str.split('/')) {
    20	    if (part === '' || part === '.') continue;
    21	    if (part === '..') {
    22	      out.pop();
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    out.push(part);
    26	  }
    27	  return (isAbs ? '/' : '') + out.join('/');
    28	}
    29	
    30	function dirname(np) {
    31	  const i = np.lastIndexOf('/');
    32	  if (i <= 0) return '/';
    33	  return np.slice(0, i);
    34	}
    35	
    36	function basename(np, parent) {
    37	  return parent === '/' ? np.slice(1) : np.slice(parent.length + 1);
    38	}
    39	
    40	export function makeMemfs(initialFiles = {}) {
    41	  const store = new Map(); // path -> string content
    42	  const dirs = new Set(['/']);
    43	  const mtimes = new Map();
    44	  const history = [];
    45	  let clock = 0;
    46	
    47	  const snapshot = () => Object.fromEntries(store);
    48	  const record = (op, paths) => history.push({ op, paths, files: snapshot() });
    49	  const touch = (np) => mtimes.set(np, ++clock);
    50	
    51	  const mkdirp = (np) => {
    52	    let cur = '';
    53	    for (const part of np.split('/').filter(Boolean)) {
    54	      cur = cur + '/' + part;
    55	      dirs.add(cur);
    56	    }
    57	  };
    58	
    59	  const ensureParent = (np, syscall) => {
    60	    const parent = dirname(np);
    61	    if (parent === '/' ) return;
    62	    if (!dirs.has(parent)) throw fsError('ENOENT', syscall, np);
    63	  };
    64	
    65	  for (const [p, content] of Object.entries(initialFiles)) {
    66	    const np = normalize(p);
    67	    mkdirp(dirname(np));
    68	    store.set(np, String(content));
    69	    touch(np);
    70	  }
    71	
    72	  const fs = {
    73	    readFileSync(p /*, enc */) {
    74	      const np = normalize(p);
    75	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'open', np);
    76	      return store.get(np);
    77	    },
    78	
    79	    writeFileSync(p, data /*, enc */) {
    80	      const np = normalize(p);
    81	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    82	      store.set(np, String(data));
    83	      touch(np);
    84	      record('writeFileSync', [np]);
    85	    },
    86	
    87	    appendFileSync(p, data) {
    88	      const np = normalize(p);
    89	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    90	      store.set(np, (store.get(np) || '') + String(data));
    91	      touch(np);
    92	      record('appendFileSync', [np]);
    93	    },
    94	
    95	    renameSync(from, to) {
    96	      const nf = normalize(from);
    97	      const nt = normalize(to);
    98	      if (store.has(nf)) {
    99	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   100	        const content = store.get(nf);
   101	        store.delete(nf);
   102	        store.set(nt, content);
   103	        touch(nt);
   104	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   105	        return;
   106	      }
   107	      // Directory rename (atomic move of the node and all descendants) — the
   108	      // lock's dead-reclaim arbitration renames the stale lock dir aside.
   109	      if (dirs.has(nf)) {
   110	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   111	        const pre = nf + '/';
   112	        dirs.delete(nf);
   113	        dirs.add(nt);
   114	        for (const k of [...store.keys()]) {
   115	          if (k.startsWith(pre)) {
   116	            store.set(nt + k.slice(nf.length), store.get(k));
   117	            store.delete(k);
   118	          }
   119	        }
   120	        for (const d of [...dirs]) {
   121	          if (d !== nt && d.startsWith(pre)) {
   122	            dirs.add(nt + d.slice(nf.length));
   123	            dirs.delete(d);
   124	          }
   125	        }
   126	        touch(nt);
   127	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   128	        return;
   129	      }
   130	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rename', nf);
   131	    },
   132	
   133	    mkdirSync(p, opts = {}) {
   134	      const np = normalize(p);
   135	      if (opts.recursive) {
   136	        mkdirp(np);
   137	        record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   138	        return undefined;
   139	      }
   140	      ensureParent(np, 'mkdir');
   141	      if (dirs.has(np) || store.has(np)) throw fsError('EEXIST', 'mkdir', np);
   142	      dirs.add(np);
   143	      record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   144	      return undefined;
   145	    },
   146	
   147	    existsSync(p) {
   148	      const np = normalize(p);
   149	      return store.has(np) || dirs.has(np);
   150	    },
   151	
   152	    readdirSync(p) {
   153	      const np = normalize(p);
   154	      if (!dirs.has(np)) {
   155	        if (store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOTDIR', 'scandir', np);
   156	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'scandir', np);
   157	      }
   158	      const names = new Set();
   159	      const collect = (k) => {
   160	        if (k !== np && dirname(k) === np) names.add(basename(k, np));
   161	      };
   162	      for (const k of store.keys()) collect(k);
   163	      for (const d of dirs) collect(d);
   164	      return [...names];
   165	    },
   166	
   167	    unlinkSync(p) {
   168	      const np = normalize(p);
   169	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'unlink', np);
   170	      store.delete(np);
   171	      record('unlinkSync', [np]);
   172	    },
   173	
   174	    rmSync(p, opts = {}) {
   175	      const np = normalize(p);
   176	      const isFile = store.has(np);
   177	      const isDir = dirs.has(np);
   178	      if (!isFile && !isDir) {
   179	        if (opts.force) return;
   180	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rm', np);
   181	      }
   182	      if (isFile) store.delete(np);
   183	      if (isDir) {
   184	        const pre = np + '/';
   185	        if (!opts.recursive) {
   186	          const hasChildren = [...store.keys(), ...dirs].some((k) => k !== np && k.startsWith(pre));
   187	          if (hasChildren) throw fsError('ENOTEMPTY', 'rmdir', np);
   188	          dirs.delete(np);
   189	        } else {
   190	          for (const k of [...store.keys()]) if (k === np || k.startsWith(pre)) store.delete(k);
   191	          for (const d of [...dirs]) if (d === np || d.startsWith(pre)) dirs.delete(d);
   192	        }
   193	      }
   194	      record('rmSync', [np]);
   195	    },
   196	
   197	    statSync(p) {
   198	      const np = normalize(p);
   199	      if (store.has(np)) {
   200	        const size = Buffer.byteLength(store.get(np));
   201	        return { isFile: () => true, isDirectory: () => false, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   202	      }
   203	      if (dirs.has(np)) {
   204	        return { isFile: () => false, isDirectory: () => true, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size: 0, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   205	      }
   206	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'stat', np);
   207	    },
   208	
   209	    // The fake cannot represent symlinks, so lstat === stat and realpath is
   210	    // normalization. Symlink-refusal behavior is proven over the real fs in
   211	    // tests/integration/symlink-escape.test.mjs.
   212	    lstatSync(p) {
   213	      return fs.statSync(p);
   214	    },
   215	
   216	    realpathSync(p) {
   217	      const np = normalize(p);
   218	      if (!store.has(np) && !dirs.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'realpath', np);
   219	      return np;
   220	    },
   221	
   222	    copyFileSync(src, dest) {
   223	      const ns = normalize(src);
   224	      const nd = normalize(dest);
   225	      if (!store.has(ns)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'copyfile', ns);
   226	      ensureParent(nd, 'copyfile');
   227	      store.set(nd, store.get(ns));
   228	      touch(nd);
   229	      record('copyFileSync', [ns, nd]);
   230	    },
   231	  };
   232	
   233	  // Introspection hook for atomicity assertions; not part of the real fs API.
   234	  fs.__history = history;
   235	
   236	  return { fs, files: () => snapshot() };
   237	}
     1	// Fake `io` object matching the injected-io contract from docs/design/core.md:
     2	// { cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }. Backed by memfs.
     3	// stdout/stderr collect writes; io.stdoutText()/io.stderrText() flatten them.
     4	// now() returns a settable ISO string (io.setNow). execFile resolves from an
     5	// execResults map keyed by the joined command string.
     6	//
     7	// Wave-B2 additions (bundle lock layer) — all optional, defaulted, and purely
     8	// additive so no prior consumer is affected:
     9	//   host / pid / startTime  — the process-identity facts the lock model writes
    10	//     into owner.json and compares on contention (see plan §Concurrency, Lock
    11	//     model iterations 3+4).
    12	//   processAlive(pid, startTime) — the "provably dead" oracle. Default: only
    13	//     THIS io's own {pid, startTime} is alive; every other descriptor reads as
    14	//     dead. Override per test to model a live foreign owner or a pid/start-time
    15	//     mismatch (reused pid).
    16	//   newFencingToken() — the injected per-acquisition token source. Deterministic
    17	//     monotonic counter ("tok-1", "tok-2", …), matching the injected-io
    18	//     discipline already used for now()/execFile so lock acquisition is
    19	//     reproducible in tests rather than drawing from Math.random.
    20	
    21	import { makeMemfs } from './memfs.mjs';
    22	
    23	export function makeIo({
    24	  files = {},
    25	  env = {},
    26	  stdin = '',
    27	  execResults = {},
    28	  now = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z',
    29	  host = 'host-A',
    30	  pid = 4242,
    31	  startTime = 111000,
    32	  processAlive = null,
    33	  fencingSeed = 1,
    34	} = {}) {
    35	  const { fs, files: filesSnapshot } = makeMemfs(files);
    36	  const outChunks = [];
    37	  const errChunks = [];
    38	  let currentNow = now;
    39	  let tokenCounter = fencingSeed - 1;
    40	  const defaultAlive = (p, s) => p === pid && s === startTime;
    41	
    42	  const stdout = { write: (s) => { outChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    43	  const stderr = { write: (s) => { errChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    44	
    45	  const execFile = (cmd, args = [], /* opts */ _opts = {}) => {
    46	    const key = [cmd, ...(Array.isArray(args) ? args : [])].join(' ');
    47	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    48	      if (!(key in execResults)) {
    49	        const e = new Error(`spawn ${cmd} ENOENT`);
    50	        e.code = 'ENOENT';
    51	        return reject(e);
    52	      }
    53	      const r = execResults[key];
    54	      const code = r.code ?? 0;
    55	      const out = r.stdout ?? '';
    56	      const err = r.stderr ?? '';
    57	      if (code !== 0) {
    58	        // util.promisify(execFile) rejects on nonzero exit; error carries code + streams.
    59	        const e = new Error(`Command failed: ${key}`);
    60	        e.code = code;
    61	        e.stdout = out;
    62	        e.stderr = err;
    63	        return reject(e);
    64	      }
    65	      resolve({ stdout: out, stderr: err });
    66	    });
    67	  };
    68	
    69	  return {
    70	    cwd: '/repo',
    71	    env,
    72	    stdin,
    73	    stdout,
    74	    stderr,
    75	    fs,
    76	    execFile,
    77	    now: () => currentNow,
    78	    setNow: (iso) => { currentNow = iso; },
    79	    host,
    80	    pid,
    81	    startTime,
    82	    processAlive: processAlive || defaultAlive,
    83	    // Tiny retry budget: unit tests exercising live-lock refusal stay fast
    84	    // while the retry code path itself still runs (production default: 120x25ms).
    85	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 2 },
    86	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${++tokenCounter}`,
    87	    stdoutText: () => outChunks.join(''),
    88	    stderrText: () => errChunks.join(''),
    89	    files: filesSnapshot,
    90	  };
    91	}

     1	const FOOTER = 'Machine-readable data: .handoff/bundle.json (baton bundle schema v1)';
     2	const DASH = '—';
     3	
     4	/**
     5	 * Render a bundle as HANDOFF.md — pure, deterministic, byte-stable. The five
     6	 * committed goldens are the format spec. Warnings are supplied by the caller
     7	 * (receive computes staleness); render never derives them.
     8	 * @param {any} bundle
     9	 * @param {{warnings?: string[]}} [options]
    10	 * @returns {string}
    11	 */
    12	export function renderHandoffMd(bundle, options = {}) {
    13	  /** @type {string[]} */
    14	  const L = [];
    15	  const push = (/** @type {string[]} */ ...lines) => L.push(...lines);

    32	  }

    45	  }

+/**
+ * Validate a regex flags string (gate-2 iter-2 B3): every char must be an
+ * allowed JS flag with no duplicates, so a flags value that compiles nowhere
+ * (`z`, `ii`) is rejected at LOAD, not left to throw later inside classify().
+ * @param {string} id @param {string} flags

+function lintFlags(id, flags) {
+  if (typeof flags !== 'string') throw new Error(`signature ${id}: regex flags must be a string`);

+  for (const c of flags) {
+    if (!ALLOWED_FLAGS.includes(c)) throw new Error(`signature ${id}: unsupported regex flag "${c}" (allowed: ${ALLOWED_FLAGS})`);

+  }
